Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. 265: +// terminalSizes sends the size of the terminal on stdout now and whenever it changes, until ctx
266: +// ends. A size that wasn't received yet is replaced by the newer one.
267: +func terminalSizes(ctx context.Context) <-chan clablabruntime.TerminalSize {
268: +	sizes := make(chan clablabruntime.TerminalSize, 1)
269: +
270: +	stdoutFd := int(os.Stdout.Fd())
271: +	if !term.IsTerminal(stdoutFd) {
272: +		return sizes
273: +	}
274: +
275: +	send := func() {
276: +		width, height, err := term.GetSize(stdoutFd)
277: +		if err != nil {
278: +			return
279: +		}
280: +
281: +		select {
282: +		case <-sizes:
283: +		default:
284: +		}
285: +		sizes <- clablabruntime.TerminalSize{Width: uint16(width), Height: uint16(height)}
286: +	}
287: +
288: +	send()
289: +
290: +	winch := make(chan os.Signal, 1)
291: +	signal.Notify(winch, syscall.SIGWINCH)
292: +
293: +	go func() {
294: +		defer signal.Stop(winch)
295: +
296: +		for {
297: +			select {
298: +			case <-ctx.Done():
299: +				return
300: +			case <-winch:
301: +				send()
302: +			}
303: +		}
304: +	}()
305: +
306: +	return sizes
307: +}

401: +
402: +// ExecAttached runs a command in the container of the one node the list options select, with
403: +// stdin, an optional TTY and terminal resizing, like docker exec -it. A command exiting nonzero
404: +// returns a *labruntime.CommandExitError. With Docker and clabernetes, a command that can't
405: +// start, such as a missing executable, returns an error without writing any output; Podman
406: +// reports its exit code. Podman also sizes the terminal from containerlab's own terminal and
407: +// ignores streams.Resize, and clabernetes applies the first size just after the command started.
408: +//
409: +// Stdin is read until it ends, which can outlast the call, so callers end it (for example by
410: +// closing a pipe) once the session is over. With Docker, the end of stdin or the detach keys
411: +// (ctrl-p, ctrl-q) end a terminal session without ending the command, which returns an error.
412: +func (c *CLab) ExecAttached(
413: +	ctx context.Context,
414: +	cmd []string,
415: +	tty bool,
416: +	streams clablabruntime.AttachStreams,
417: +	listOptions ...ListOption,
418: +) error {
419: +	if len(cmd) == 0 {
420: +		return errors.New("an attached exec needs a command")
421: +	}
422: +
423: +	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
424: +		containers, err := c.ListLabRuntimeContainers(ctx, false, listOptions...)
425: +		if err != nil {
426: +			return err
427: +		}
428: +
429: +		target, err := attachedExecTarget(containers)
430: +		if err != nil {
431: +			return err
432: +		}
433: +
434: +		namespace, labName, nodeName, err := labRuntimeContainerParts(*target)
435: +		if err != nil {
436: +			return fmt.Errorf("exec target %s is invalid: %w", target.Names[0], err)
437: +		}
438: +
439: +		return c.AttachLabRuntimeNode(ctx, clablabruntime.AttachRequest{
440: +			Name:      labName,
441: +			Namespace: namespace,
442: +			NodeName:  nodeName,
443: +			Command:   cmd,
444: +			TTY:       tty,
445: +		}, streams)
446: +	}
447: +
448: +	containers, err := c.ListContainers(ctx, listOptions...)
449: +	if err != nil {
450: +		return err
451: +	}
452: +
453: +	target, err := attachedExecTarget(containers)
454: +	if err != nil {
455: +		return err
456: +	}
457: +
458: +	attacher, ok := target.Runtime.(clabruntime.ContainerAttacher)
459: +	if !ok {
460: +		return fmt.Errorf("container runtime %q does not support attached exec",
461: +			target.Runtime.GetName())
462: +	}
463: +
464: +	return attacher.AttachContainer(ctx, target.Names[0], cmd, tty, streams)
465: +}
466: +
467: +// attachedExecTarget returns the container of an attached exec, which needs the filter to match
468: +// exactly one.
469: +func attachedExecTarget(
470: +	containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer,
471: +) (*clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
472: +	switch len(containers) {
473: +	case 0:
474: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("filter did not match any containers")
475: +	case 1:
476: +		return &containers[0], nil
477: +	}
478: +
479: +	return nil, fmt.Errorf(
480: +		"an attached exec needs a filter matching exactly one container, the filter matched %d",
481: +		len(containers))
482: +}

507: +// attachingLabRuntime records the attach request of an attached exec on a lab runtime.
508: +type attachingLabRuntime struct {
509: +	clablabruntime.LabRuntime
510: +
511: +	request clablabruntime.AttachRequest
512: +	exitErr error
513: +}
514: +
515: +func (r *attachingLabRuntime) Inspect(
516: +	context.Context,
517: +	clablabruntime.InspectRequest,
518: +) (*clablabruntime.LabState, error) {
519: +	return &clablabruntime.LabState{
520: +		Name:      "lab1",
521: +		Namespace: "lab-ns",
522: +		Nodes:     []clablabruntime.NodeState{{Name: "node1"}, {Name: "node2"}},
523: +	}, nil
524: +}
525: +
526: +func (r *attachingLabRuntime) Attach(
527: +	_ context.Context,
528: +	req clablabruntime.AttachRequest,
529: +	streams clablabruntime.AttachStreams,
530: +) error {
531: +	r.request = req
532: +
533: +	if _, err := io.Copy(streams.Stdout, streams.Stdin); err != nil {
534: +		return err
535: +	}
536: +
537: +	return r.exitErr
538: +}

540: +func TestExecAttachedWithALabRuntime(t *testing.T) {
541: +	t.Parallel()
542: +
543: +	runtime := &attachingLabRuntime{exitErr: &clablabruntime.CommandExitError{Code: 3}}
544: +	c := &CLab{
545: +		Config:             &Config{Name: "lab1", Topology: clabtypes.NewTopology()},
546: +		TopoPaths:          &clabtypes.TopoPaths{},

549: +		labRuntimeIdentity: testIdentity,
550: +	}
551: +

553: +	err := c.ExecAttached(context.Background(), []string{"sh", "-c", "cat > /tmp/f"}, true,
554: +		clablabruntime.AttachStreams{Stdin: strings.NewReader("config"), Stdout: &stdout},
555: +		WithListFromCliArgs([]string{clabconstants.NodeName + "=node1"}))
556: +
557: +	var exitErr *clablabruntime.CommandExitError
558: +	if !errors.As(err, &exitErr) || exitErr.Code != 3 {
559: +		t.Fatalf("ExecAttached() error = %v, want the command's exit code 3", err)
560: +	}
561: +	want := clablabruntime.AttachRequest{
562: +		Name:      "lab1",
563: +		Namespace: "lab-ns",
564: +		NodeName:  "node1",

566: +		TTY:       true,
567: +		Identity:  testIdentity,
568: +	}
569: +	if d := cmp.Diff(want, runtime.request); d != "" {
570: +		t.Fatalf("attach request mismatch (-want +got):\n%s", d)
571: +	}
572: +	if stdout.String() != "config" {
573: +		t.Fatalf("stdin reached the command as %q", stdout.String())
574: +	}
575: +
576: +	err = c.ExecAttached(context.Background(), []string{"sh"}, false, clablabruntime.AttachStreams{})
577: +	if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "matched 2") {
578: +		t.Fatalf("ExecAttached() on two nodes error = %v", err)
579: +	}
580: +}

591: +func (r *attachingRuntime) AttachContainer(
592: +	_ context.Context,

600: +	r.tty = tty

673: diff --git a/docs/cmd/exec.md b/docs/cmd/exec.md
674: index 600a7e8..1a047a0 100644
675: --- a/docs/cmd/exec.md
676: +++ b/docs/cmd/exec.md
677: @@ -38,10 +38,22 @@ The command to be executed on the nodes is provided with `--cmd` flag. The comma
678:  
679:  The `--format | -f` flag allows selecting between plain text format output or a json variant. Consult with the examples below to see the differences between these two formatting options.
680:  
681:  Defaults to `plain` output format.
682:  
683: +### stdin
684: +
685: +With the `--stdin | -i` flag the command gets containerlab's stdin, like `docker exec -i`, for example to copy a file into a node. The output of the command is written to stdout and stderr as it comes, and containerlab exits with the command's exit code. A command that can't start, such as a missing executable, is reported as an error with the Docker and clabernetes runtimes, and containerlab exits with 1.
686: +
687: +The filter must then match exactly one node, and `--cmd` can be given once. `--format` is not supported with `--stdin` and `--tty`.
688: +
689: +### tty
690: +
691: +The `--tty` flag runs the command in a terminal, like `docker exec -t`; its output goes to stdout. Together with `--stdin` it opens an interactive session, for which containerlab's stdin must be a terminal. The same rules as for `--stdin` apply.
692: +
693: +With Docker, the detach keys `ctrl-p`, `ctrl-q` leave an interactive session like with `docker exec`; the command keeps running and containerlab exits with an error. With Podman, `--tty` from a terminal needs `--stdin`, as Podman connects the session to containerlab's terminal.
694: +
695:  ### label
696:  
697:  Using `--label` it is possible to filter the nodes to execute the command on using labels attached to the nodes. The label is provided as a string in the form of `key=value`. The `key` is the label name, and the `value` is the label value.
698:  
699:  Exec command should either be provided with a topology file, or labels, or both.
700: @@ -93,10 +105,23 @@ INFO[0000] Executed command "ip -4 addr show dummy-mgmt0" on the node "srl". std
701:  2: dummy-mgmt0: <BROADCAST,NOARP> mtu 1500 qdisc noop state DOWN group default qlen 1000
702:      inet 172.20.20.3/24 brd 172.20.20.255 scope global dummy-mgmt0
703:         valid_lft forever preferred_lft forever 
704:  ```
705:  
706: +### Open an interactive shell on a node
707: +
708: +```bash
709: +containerlab exec -t srl02.clab.yml --label clab-node-name=srl1 --stdin --tty --cmd bash
710: +```
711: +
712: +### Copy a file into a node
713: +
714: +```bash
715: +containerlab exec -t srl02.clab.yml --label clab-node-name=srl1 --stdin \
716: +  --cmd "sh -c 'cat > /tmp/config.sh'" < config.sh
717: +```
718: +
719:  ### Execute a CLI Command
720:  
721:  ```bash
722:  ❯ containerlab exec -t srl02.yml --cmd 'sr_cli  "show version"'
723:  INFO[0001] clab-srl02-srl1: stdout:
724: diff --git a/docs/manual/clabernetes/runtime.md b/docs/manual/clabernetes/runtime.md
725: index 751ccf1..0b8e1b8 100644
726: --- a/docs/manual/clabernetes/runtime.md
727: +++ b/docs/manual/clabernetes/runtime.md
728: @@ -614,10 +614,18 @@ needs to reach the cluster.
729:  If any selected command returns nonzero, or pod exec itself fails, the outer
730:  `containerlab exec` also returns nonzero. Successful and failed results that
731:  were received are still printed, so automation can use both the output and the
732:  process exit status.
733:  
734: +With `--stdin` and `--tty`, `exec` attaches containerlab's stdin and a terminal
735: +to the command in the device container of one node, like `kubectl exec -it`,
736: +and exits with the command's exit code:
737: +
738: +```bash
739: +containerlab --runtime c9s exec -t topo.clab.yml --label clab-node-name=srl1 --stdin --tty --cmd bash
740: +```

812: +
813: +// fakeExecAPI serves the exec endpoints of the Docker API. The exec session echoes its stdin
814: +// and exits with exitCode.
815: +type fakeExecAPI struct {
816: +	exitCode int
817: +	// notStarted makes the exec fail to start, like a missing executable
818: +	notStarted bool
819: +	// running reports the command as still running after the session ended
820: +	running bool
821: +	// endless writes output until the client closes the connection
822: +	endless bool
823: +
824: +	mu      sync.Mutex
825: +	options container.ExecOptions
826: +	resized []string
827: +}
828: +
829: +func (f *fakeExecAPI) ServeHTTP(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
830: +	switch {
831: +	case strings.HasSuffix(r.URL.Path, "/containers/clab-lab1-r1/exec"):
832: +		f.mu.Lock()
833: +		_ = json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&f.options)
834: +		f.mu.Unlock()
835: +		_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]any{"Id": "exec1"})
836: +	case strings.HasSuffix(r.URL.Path, "/exec/exec1/start"):
837: +		_, _ = io.Copy(io.Discard, r.Body)
838: +		f.echo(w)
839: +	case strings.HasSuffix(r.URL.Path, "/exec/exec1/resize"):
840: +		f.mu.Lock()
841: +		f.resized = append(f.resized, r.URL.Query().Get("h")+"x"+r.URL.Query().Get("w"))
842: +		f.mu.Unlock()
843: +	case strings.HasSuffix(r.URL.Path, "/exec/exec1/json"):
844: +		pid := 42
845: +		if f.notStarted {
846: +			pid = 0
847: +		}
848: +		_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]any{
849: +			"ExitCode": f.exitCode, "Pid": pid, "Running": f.running,
850: +		})
851: +	default:
852: +		http.NotFound(w, r)
853: +	}
854: +}
855: +
856: +// echo upgrades the connection like the exec start endpoint and writes back what it reads until
857: +// the client closes its write side, multiplexed unless the session has a TTY.
858: +func (f *fakeExecAPI) echo(w http.ResponseWriter) {
859: +	conn, rw, err := w.(http.Hijacker).Hijack()
860: +	if err != nil {
861: +		return
862: +	}
863: +	defer conn.Close()
864: +
865: +	_, _ = rw.WriteString("HTTP/1.1 101 UPGRADED\r\n" +
866: +		"Content-Type: application/vnd.docker.raw-stream\r\n" +
867: +		"Connection: Upgrade\r\nUpgrade: tcp\r\n\r\n")
868: +	_ = rw.Flush()
869: +
870: +	if f.notStarted {
871: +		_, _ = conn.Write([]byte("OCI runtime exec failed: exec: \"nope\": executable file not found\r\n"))
872: +		return
873: +	}
874: +
875: +	if f.endless {
876: +		for {
877: +			if _, err := conn.Write([]byte("x")); err != nil {
878: +				return
879: +			}
880: +			time.Sleep(time.Millisecond)
881: +		}
882: +	}
883: +
884: +	f.mu.Lock()
885: +	tty, attachStdin := f.options.Tty, f.options.AttachStdin
886: +	f.mu.Unlock()
887: +
888: +	var input []byte
889: +	if attachStdin {
890: +		input, _ = io.ReadAll(rw)
891: +	}
892: +
893: +	if tty {
894: +		_, _ = conn.Write(input)
895: +		return
896: +	}
897: +	_, _ = stdcopy.NewStdWriter(conn, stdcopy.Stdout).Write(input)
898: +	_, _ = stdcopy.NewStdWriter(conn, stdcopy.Stderr).Write([]byte("err"))
899: +}
900: +
901: +func newFakeExecRuntime(t *testing.T, api *fakeExecAPI) *DockerRuntime {
902: +	t.Helper()
903: +
904: +	srv := httptest.NewServer(api)
905: +	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
906: +
907: +	cli, err := dockerC.NewClientWithOpts(
908: +		dockerC.WithHost("tcp://"+srv.Listener.Addr().String()),
909: +		dockerC.WithVersion("1.45"),
910: +	)
911: +	if err != nil {
912: +		t.Fatal(err)
913: +	}
914: +
915: +	return &DockerRuntime{Client: cli}
916: +}
917: +
918: +func TestAttachContainer(t *testing.T) {
919: +	api := &fakeExecAPI{}
920: +	d := newFakeExecRuntime(t, api)
921: +
922: +	var stdout, stderr strings.Builder
923: +	err := d.AttachContainer(context.Background(), "clab-lab1-r1",
924: +		[]string{"sh", "-c", "cat > /tmp/f"}, false, clablabruntime.AttachStreams{
925: +			Stdin:  strings.NewReader("config"),
926: +			Stdout: &stdout,
927: +			Stderr: &stderr,
928: +		})
929: +	if err != nil {
930: +		t.Fatal(err)
931: +	}
932: +
933: +	if !api.options.AttachStdin || !api.options.AttachStdout || !api.options.AttachStderr ||
934: +		api.options.Tty || strings.Join(api.options.Cmd, "|") != "sh|-c|cat > /tmp/f" {
935: +		t.Fatalf("exec options = %+v", api.options)
936: +	}
937: +	if stdout.String() != "config" || stderr.String() != "err" {
938: +		t.Fatalf("stdout %q stderr %q", stdout.String(), stderr.String())
939: +	}
940: +}
941: +
942: +func TestAttachContainerWithATTY(t *testing.T) {
943: +	api := &fakeExecAPI{exitCode: 3}
944: +	d := newFakeExecRuntime(t, api)
945: +
946: +	resize := make(chan clablabruntime.TerminalSize, 1)
947: +	resize <- clablabruntime.TerminalSize{Width: 101, Height: 33}
948: +
949: +	stdin, input := io.Pipe()
950: +	var stdout strings.Builder
951: +	done := make(chan error, 1)
952: +	go func() {
953: +		done <- d.AttachContainer(context.Background(), "clab-lab1-r1", []string{"sh"}, true,
954: +			clablabruntime.AttachStreams{Stdin: stdin, Stdout: &stdout, Resize: resize})
955: +	}()
956: +
957: +	// the size sent before the session starts is its initial size, later sizes resize it
958: +	resize <- clablabruntime.TerminalSize{Width: 120, Height: 40}
959: +	waitForResizes(t, api, []string{"40x120"})
960: +
961: +	_, _ = input.Write([]byte("exit 3\n"))
962: +	_ = input.Close()
963: +
964: +	var exitErr *clablabruntime.CommandExitError
965: +	if err := <-done; !errors.As(err, &exitErr) || exitErr.Code != 3 {
966: +		t.Fatalf("AttachContainer() error = %v, want the command's exit code 3", err)
967: +	}
968: +	if !api.options.Tty || !api.options.AttachStdout || !api.options.AttachStderr {
969: +		t.Fatalf("exec options = %+v", api.options)
970: +	}
971: +	if api.options.ConsoleSize == nil || *api.options.ConsoleSize != [2]uint{33, 101} {
972: +		t.Fatalf("console size = %v, want [33 101]", api.options.ConsoleSize)
973: +	}
974: +	if stdout.String() != "exit 3\n" {
975: +		t.Fatalf("stdout %q", stdout.String())
976: +	}
977: +}
978: +
979: +func waitForResizes(t *testing.T, api *fakeExecAPI, want []string) {
980: +	t.Helper()
981: +
982: +	var resized []string
983: +	for deadline := time.Now().Add(5 * time.Second); time.Now().Before(deadline); {
984: +		api.mu.Lock()
985: +		resized = slices.Clone(api.resized)
986: +		api.mu.Unlock()
987: +		if len(resized) >= len(want) {
988: +			break
989: +		}
990: +		time.Sleep(10 * time.Millisecond)
991: +	}
992: +	if !slices.Equal(resized, want) {
993: +		t.Fatalf("terminal resizes = %q, want %q", resized, want)
994: +	}
995: +}
996: +
997: +func TestAttachContainerWithoutOutputStreams(t *testing.T) {
998: +	api := &fakeExecAPI{exitCode: 2}
999: +	d := newFakeExecRuntime(t, api)
1000: +
1001: +	err := d.AttachContainer(context.Background(), "clab-lab1-r1", []string{"false"}, false,
1002: +		clablabruntime.AttachStreams{})
1003: +
1004: +	// without attached output Docker would end the session before the command exits
1005: +	var exitErr *clablabruntime.CommandExitError
1006: +	if !errors.As(err, &exitErr) || exitErr.Code != 2 {
1007: +		t.Fatalf("AttachContainer() error = %v, want the command's exit code 2", err)
1008: +	}
1009: +	if api.options.AttachStdin || !api.options.AttachStdout || !api.options.AttachStderr {
1010: +		t.Fatalf("exec options = %+v", api.options)
1011: +	}
1012: +}

1034: +func TestAttachContainerEndsWithTheContext(t *testing.T) {
1035: +	api := &fakeExecAPI{endless: true}
1036: +	d := newFakeExecRuntime(t, api)
1037: +
1038: +	resize := make(chan clablabruntime.TerminalSize, 1)
1039: +	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
1040: +	stdout := &afterReturnWriter{firstWrite: make(chan struct{})}
1041: +	go func() {
1042: +		<-stdout.firstWrite
1043: +		cancel()
1044: +	}()
1045: +
1046: +	err := d.AttachContainer(ctx, "clab-lab1-r1", []string{"sh"}, true,
1047: +		clablabruntime.AttachStreams{Stdout: stdout, Resize: resize})
1048: +	stdout.returned.Store(true)
1049: +
1050: +	if !errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) {
1051: +		t.Fatalf("AttachContainer() error = %v, want context.Canceled", err)
1052: +	}
1053: +
1054: +	// no new output or resizes reach the caller or the session after the call returned, only a
1055: +	// write that was already in progress
1056: +	resize <- clablabruntime.TerminalSize{Width: 120, Height: 40}
1057: +	time.Sleep(100 * time.Millisecond)
1058: +	if stdout.lateWrites.Load() > 1 {
1059: +		t.Fatalf("%d writes after AttachContainer returned", stdout.lateWrites.Load())
1060: +	}
1061: +	api.mu.Lock()
1062: +	defer api.mu.Unlock()
1063: +	if len(api.resized) != 0 {
1064: +		t.Fatalf("terminal resizes = %q after AttachContainer returned", api.resized)
1065: +	}
1066: +}
1067: +
1068: +// afterReturnWriter signals its first write and counts the writes after returned is set.
1069: +type afterReturnWriter struct {
1070: +	firstWrite chan struct{}
1071: +	once       sync.Once
1072: +	returned   atomic.Bool
1073: +	lateWrites atomic.Int32
1074: +}
1075: +
1076: +func (w *afterReturnWriter) Write(p []byte) (int, error) {
1077: +	w.once.Do(func() { close(w.firstWrite) })
1078: +	if w.returned.Load() {
1079: +		w.lateWrites.Add(1)
1080: +	}
1081: +
1082: +	return len(p), nil
1083: +}

1138: +// AttachContainer runs an interactive command in a container through a Docker exec session,
1139: +// like docker exec -it.
1140: +func (d *DockerRuntime) AttachContainer(
1141: +	ctx context.Context,
1142: +	containerName string,
1143: +	cmd []string,
1144: +	tty bool,
1145: +	streams clablabruntime.AttachStreams,
1146: +) error {
1147: +	// resizing ends with the session
1148: +	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(ctx)
1149: +	defer cancel()
1150: +
1151: +	// the terminal starts with the size that was already sent, for commands that read it once
1152: +	var consoleSize *[2]uint
1153: +	if tty && streams.Resize != nil {
1154: +		select {
1155: +		case size, ok := <-streams.Resize:
1156: +			if ok {
1157: +				consoleSize = &[2]uint{uint(size.Height), uint(size.Width)}
1158: +			}
1159: +		default:
1160: +		}
1161: +	}
1162: +
1163: +	// the output is always attached, without it Docker ends the session before the command
1164: +	// exits
1165: +	execID, err := d.Client.ContainerExecCreate(ctx, containerName, container.ExecOptions{
1166: +		User:         "0", // root
1167: +		Tty:          tty,
1168: +		ConsoleSize:  consoleSize,
1169: +		AttachStdin:  streams.Stdin != nil,
1170: +		AttachStdout: true,
1171: +		AttachStderr: true,
1172: +		Cmd:          cmd,
1173: +	})
1174: +	if err != nil {
1175: +		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create exec in container %q: %w", containerName, err)
1176: +	}
1177: +
1178: +	rsp, err := d.Client.ContainerExecAttach(ctx, execID.ID, container.ExecStartOptions{Tty: tty})
1179: +	if err != nil {
1180: +		return fmt.Errorf("failed to attach to exec in container %q: %w", containerName, err)
1181: +	}
1182: +	defer rsp.Close()
1183: +
1184: +	if tty && streams.Resize != nil {
1185: +		go d.resizeExec(ctx, execID.ID, streams.Resize)
1186: +	}
1187: +
1188: +	if streams.Stdin != nil {
1189: +		stdin := streams.Stdin
1190: +		if tty {
1191: +			// the detach keys end the input here, like docker exec does, so that Docker ends the
1192: +			// session the same way as when stdin ends
1193: +			stdin = dockerterm.NewEscapeProxy(stdin, detachKeys)
1194: +		}
1195: +
1196: +		go func() {
1197: +			_, _ = io.Copy(rsp.Conn, stdin)
1198: +			// the end of the input closes the command's stdin
1199: +			_ = rsp.CloseWrite()
1200: +		}()
1201: +	}
1202: +
1203: +	stdout, stderr := streams.Stdout, streams.Stderr
1204: +	if stdout == nil {
1205: +		stdout = io.Discard
1206: +	}
1207: +	if stderr == nil {
1208: +		stderr = io.Discard
1209: +	}
1210: +
1211: +	start := &execStartCheck{inspect: func() (container.ExecInspect, error) {
1212: +		return d.Client.ContainerExecInspect(ctx, execID.ID)
1213: +	}}
1214: +
1215: +	outputDone := make(chan error, 1)
1216: +	go func() {
1217: +		var err error
1218: +		if tty {
1219: +			_, err = io.Copy(start.writer(stdout), rsp.Reader)
1220: +		} else {
1221: +			_, err = stdcopy.StdCopy(start.writer(stdout), start.writer(stderr), rsp.Reader)
1222: +		}
1223: +		outputDone <- err
1224: +	}()
1225: +
1226: +	select {
1227: +	case err := <-outputDone:
1228: +		if err != nil {
1229: +			return fmt.Errorf("failed to read exec output of container %q: %w", containerName, err)
1230: +		}
1231: +	case <-ctx.Done():
1232: +		// no output is written after the call returns
1233: +		start.end()
1234: +
1235: +		return ctx.Err()
1236: +	}
1237: +
1238: +	execInspect, err := d.Client.ContainerExecInspect(ctx, execID.ID)
1239: +	if err != nil {
1240: +		return err
1241: +	}
1242: +	if failed, reason := start.result(); failed || notStarted(execInspect) {
1243: +		err := fmt.Errorf("command %q failed to start in container %q",
1244: +			strings.Join(cmd, " "), containerName)
1245: +		if reason := strings.TrimSpace(reason); reason != "" {
1246: +			err = fmt.Errorf("%w: %s", err, reason)
1247: +		}
1248: +
1249: +		return err
1250: +	}
1251: +	if execInspect.Running {
1252: +		// Docker ends the output of a terminal session when its stdin ends or on the detach
1253: +		// keys, without ending the command
1254: +		return fmt.Errorf("detached from command %q in container %q, which is still running",
1255: +			strings.Join(cmd, " "), containerName)
1256: +	}
1257: +	if execInspect.ExitCode != 0 {
1258: +		return &clablabruntime.CommandExitError{Code: execInspect.ExitCode}
1259: +	}
1260: +
1261: +	return nil
1262: +}
1263: +
1264: +// detachKeys are Docker's default detach keys, ctrl-p ctrl-q.
1265: +var detachKeys = []byte{0x10, 0x11} //nolint: gochecknoglobals
1266: +
1267: +// notStarted tells whether an exec ended without its process ever running, such as a missing
1268: +// executable.
1269: +func notStarted(inspect container.ExecInspect) bool {
1270: +	return !inspect.Running && inspect.Pid == 0 && inspect.ExitCode != 0
1271: +}
1272: +
1273: +// execStartCheck holds back the first output of an exec until Docker reports whether the command
1274: +// started. Docker writes why a command didn't start into the output, which becomes the error
1275: +// instead. Output after the session ended is dropped.
1276: +type execStartCheck struct {
1277: +	inspect func() (container.ExecInspect, error)
1278: +
1279: +	mu      sync.Mutex
1280: +	checked bool
1281: +	failed  bool
1282: +	reason  []byte
1283: +	ended   bool
1284: +}
1285: +
1286: +func (c *execStartCheck) end() {
1287: +	c.mu.Lock()
1288: +	defer c.mu.Unlock()
1289: +
1290: +	c.ended = true
1291: +}
1292: +
1293: +func (c *execStartCheck) writer(w io.Writer) io.Writer {
1294: +	return execStartWriter{check: c, w: w}
1295: +}
1296: +
1297: +func (c *execStartCheck) result() (bool, string) {
1298: +	c.mu.Lock()
1299: +	defer c.mu.Unlock()
1300: +
1301: +	return c.failed, string(c.reason)
1302: +}
1303: +
1304: +// hold tells whether output must be held back or dropped, checking the exec on the first output.
1305: +func (c *execStartCheck) hold(p []byte) bool {
1306: +	c.mu.Lock()
1307: +	defer c.mu.Unlock()
1308: +
1309: +	if c.ended {
1310: +		return true
1311: +	}
1312: +	if !c.checked {
1313: +		c.checked = true
1314: +		if inspect, err := c.inspect(); err == nil && !inspect.Running && inspect.Pid == 0 {
1315: +			c.failed = true
1316: +		}
1317: +	}
1318: +	if c.failed {
1319: +		c.reason = append(c.reason, p...)
1320: +	}
1321: +
1322: +	return c.failed
1323: +}
1324: +
1325: +type execStartWriter struct {
1326: +	check *execStartCheck
1327: +	w     io.Writer
1328: +}
1329: +
1330: +func (w execStartWriter) Write(p []byte) (int, error) {
1331: +	if w.check.hold(p) {
1332: +		return len(p), nil
1333: +	}
1334: +
1335: +	return w.w.Write(p)
1336: +}
1337: +
1338: +// resizeExec applies terminal size changes to an exec session until resize is closed or ctx
1339: +// ends.
1340: +func (d *DockerRuntime) resizeExec(
1341: +	ctx context.Context,
1342: +	execID string,
1343: +	resize <-chan clablabruntime.TerminalSize,
1344: +) {
1345: +	for {
1346: +		select {
1347: +		case <-ctx.Done():
1348: +			return
1349: +		case size, ok := <-resize:
1350: +			if !ok || ctx.Err() != nil {
1351: +				return
1352: +			}
1353: +			err := d.Client.ContainerExecResize(ctx, execID, container.ResizeOptions{
1354: +				Height: uint(size.Height),
1355: +				Width:  uint(size.Width),
1356: +			})
1357: +			if err != nil {
1358: +				log.Debugf("failed to resize the terminal of exec %s: %v", execID, err)
1359: +			}
1360: +		}
1361: +	}
1362: +}

The output of the command is written to stdout and stderr as it comes, and containerlab exits with the command's exit code. A command that can't start, such as a missing executable, is reported as an error with the Docker and clabernetes runtimes, and containerlab exits with 1.\n686: +\n687: +The filter must then match exactly one node, and `--cmd` can be given once. `--format` is not supported with `--stdin` and `--tty`.\n688: +\n689: +### tty\n690: +\n691: +The `--tty` flag runs the command in a terminal, like `docker exec -t`; its output goes to stdout. Together with `--stdin` it opens an interactive session, for which containerlab's stdin must be a terminal. The same rules as for `--stdin` apply.\n692: +\n693: +With Docker, the detach keys `ctrl-p`, `ctrl-q` leave an interactive session like with `docker exec`; the command keeps running and containerlab exits with an error. The comma\n \n The `--format | -f` flag allows selecting between plain text format output or a json variant. Consult with the examples below to see the differences between these two formatting options.\n \n Defaults to `plain` output format.\n \n+### stdin\n+\n+With the `--stdin | -i` flag the command gets containerlab's stdin, like `docker exec -i`, for example to copy a file into a node. The output of the command is written to stdout and stderr as it comes, and containerlab exits with the command's exit code. A command that can't start, such as a missing executable, is reported as an error with the Docker and clabernetes runtimes, and containerlab exits with 1.\n+\n+The filter must then match exactly one node, and `--cmd` can be given once. `--format` is not supported with `--stdin` and `--tty`.\n+\n+### tty\n+\n+The `--tty` flag runs the command in a terminal, like `docker exec -t`; its output goes to stdout. Together with `--stdin` it opens an interactive session, for which containerlab's stdin must be a terminal. The same rules as for `--stdin` apply.\n+\n+With Docker, the detach keys `ctrl-p`, `ctrl-q` leave an interactive session like with `docker exec`; the command keeps running and containerlab exits with an error. With Podman, `--tty` from a terminal needs `--stdin`, as Podman connects the session to containerlab's terminal.\n+\n ### label\n \n Using `--label` it is possible to filter the nodes to execute the command on using labels attached to the nodes. 23: func execCmd(o *Options) (*cobra.Command, error) {
24: 	c := &cobra.Command{
25: 		Use:   "exec",
26: 		Short: "execute a command in one or multiple containers",
27: 		RunE: func(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
28: 			return execFn(cobraCmd, o)
29: 		},
30: 	}
31: 
32: 	c.Flags().StringArrayVarP(
33: 		&o.Exec.Commands,
34: 		"cmd",
35: 		"",
36: 		o.Exec.Commands,
37: 		"command to execute",
38: 	)
39: 	c.Flags().StringSliceVarP(
40: 		&o.Filter.LabelFilter,
41: 		"label",
42: 		"",
43: 		o.Filter.LabelFilter,
44: 		"labels to filter container subset",
45: 	)
46: 	c.Flags().StringVarP(
47: 		&o.Exec.Format,
48: 		"format",
49: 		"f",
50: 		o.Exec.Format,
51: 		"output format. One of [json, plain]",
52: 	)
53: 	c.Flags().BoolVarP(
54: 		&o.Exec.Stdin,
55: 		"stdin",
56: 		"i",
57: 		o.Exec.Stdin,
58: 		"pass stdin to the command; the filter must match exactly one node",
59: 	)
60: 	c.Flags().BoolVar(
61: 		&o.Exec.TTY,
62: 		"tty",
63: 		o.Exec.TTY,
64: 		"run the command in a terminal; the filter must match exactly one node",
65: 	)
66: 
67: 	c.Example = `# Show the addresses of all nodes of the lab
68: containerlab exec -t srl02.clab.yml --cmd 'ip -4 addr'
69: 
70: # Open an interactive shell on node srl1
71: containerlab exec -t srl02.clab.yml --label clab-node-name=srl1 -i --tty --cmd bash
72: 
73: # Copy a file into node srl1
74: containerlab exec -t srl02.clab.yml --label clab-node-name=srl1 -i \
75:   --cmd "sh -c 'cat > /tmp/config.sh'" < config.sh`
76: 
77: 	return c, nil
78: }
79: 
80: func execFn(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, o *Options) error {
81: 	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(cobraCmd.Context())
82: 	defer cancel()
83: 
84: 	if len(o.Exec.Commands) == 0 {
85: 		return errors.New("provide command to execute")
86: 	}
87: 
88: 	if o.Exec.Stdin || o.Exec.TTY {
89: 		if len(o.Exec.Commands) != 1 {
90: 			return errors.New("the --stdin and --tty flags run exactly one command")
91: 		}
92: 		if cobraCmd.Flags().Changed("format") {
93: 			return errors.New("the --format flag is not supported with --stdin and --tty")
94: 		}
95: 	}
96: 
97: 	outputFormat, err := clabexec.ParseExecOutputFormat(o.Exec.Format)
98: 	if err != nil {
99: 		return err
100: 	}
101: 
102: 	c, err := clabcore.NewContainerLab(o.ToClabOptions()...)
103: 	if err != nil {
104: 		return err
105: 	}
106: 
107: 	err = c.CheckConnectivity(ctx)
108: 	if err != nil {
109: 		return err
110: 	}
111: 
112: 	listOptions := []clabcore.ListOption{
113: 		clabcore.WithListFromCliArgs(o.Filter.LabelFilter),
114: 	}
115: 
116: 	if o.Global.TopologyFile != "" {
117: 		listOptions = append(
118: 			listOptions,
119: 			clabcore.WithListLabName(c.Config.Name),
120: 		)
121: 	}
122: 
123: 	if o.Exec.Stdin || o.Exec.TTY {
124: 		return execAttachedFn(ctx, cobraCmd, o, c, listOptions)
125: 	}
126: 
127: 	resultCollection, err := c.Exec(ctx, o.Exec.Commands, listOptions...)
128: 	if resultCollection != nil {
129: 		switch outputFormat {
130: 		case clabconstants.FormatPlain:
131: 			resultCollection.Log()
132: 		case clabconstants.FormatJSON:
133: 			out, dumpErr := resultCollection.Dump(outputFormat)
134: 			if dumpErr != nil {
135: 				return fmt.Errorf("failed to print the results collection: %v", dumpErr)
136: 			}
137: 
138: 			fmt.Println(out)
139: 		}
140: 	}
141: 
142: 	return err
143: }
144: 
145: // execAttachedFn runs the command with stdin and a terminal attached, as the flags ask for.
146: func execAttachedFn(
147: 	ctx context.Context,
148: 	cobraCmd *cobra.Command,
149: 	o *Options,
150: 	c *clabcore.CLab,
151: 	listOptions []clabcore.ListOption,
152: ) error {
153: 	streams := clablabruntime.AttachStreams{
154: 		Stdout: cobraCmd.OutOrStdout(),
155: 		Stderr: cobraCmd.ErrOrStderr(),
156: 	}
157: 
158: 	if o.Exec.Stdin {
159: 		streams.Stdin = cobraCmd.InOrStdin()
160: 	}
161: 
162: 	restoreTerminal := func() {}
163: 
164: 	if o.Exec.TTY {
165: 		stdinFd := int(os.Stdin.Fd())
166: 
167: 		if o.Exec.Stdin {
168: 			if !term.IsTerminal(stdinFd) {
169: 				return errors.New("the input device is not a TTY; use --stdin without --tty")
170: 			}
171: 
172: 			oldState, err := term.MakeRaw(stdinFd)
173: 			if err != nil {
174: 				return fmt.Errorf("failed to set the terminal to raw mode: %w", err)
175: 			}
176: 			restoreTerminal = func() { _ = term.Restore(stdinFd, oldState) }
177: 			defer restoreTerminal()
178: 
179: 			restoreOnSignalExit.Store(&restoreTerminal)
180: 			defer restoreOnSignalExit.Store(nil)
181: 		}
182: 
183: 		streams.Resize = terminalSizes(ctx)
184: 	}
185: 
186: 	execCmd, err := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromString(o.Exec.Commands[0])
187: 	if err != nil {
188: 		return err
189: 	}
190: 
191: 	err = c.ExecAttached(ctx, execCmd.GetCmd(), o.Exec.TTY, streams, listOptions...)
192: 
193: 	var exitErr *clablabruntime.CommandExitError
194: 	if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
195: 		// exit with the command's exit code, like docker exec, without an error message or the
196: 		// terminal queries of printing one, whose answers the stdin copy would swallow
197: 		restoreTerminal()
198: 		os.Exit(exitErr.Code)
199: 	}
200: 
201: 	if err != nil && o.Exec.Stdin && term.IsTerminal(int(os.Stdin.Fd())) {
202: 		// the stdin copy still reads the terminal, so the error is printed without those queries
203: 		restoreTerminal()
204: 		fmt.Fprintln(cobraCmd.ErrOrStderr(), "Error:", err)
205: 		os.Exit(1)
206: 	}
207: 
208: 	return err
209: }
210: 
211: // terminalSizes sends the size of the terminal on stdout now and whenever it changes, until ctx
212: // ends. A size that wasn't received yet is replaced by the newer one.
213: func terminalSizes(ctx context.Context) <-chan clablabruntime.TerminalSize {
214: 	sizes := make(chan clablabruntime.TerminalSize, 1)
215: 
216: 	stdoutFd := int(os.Stdout.Fd())
217: 	if !term.IsTerminal(stdoutFd) {
218: 		return sizes
219: 	}
220: 
221: 	send := func() {
222: 		width, height, err := term.GetSize(stdoutFd)
223: 		if err != nil {
224: 			return
225: 		}
226: 
227: 		select {
228: 		case <-sizes:
229: 		default:
230: 		}
231: 		sizes <- clablabruntime.TerminalSize{Width: uint16(width), Height: uint16(height)}
232: 	}
233: 
234: 	send()
235: 
236: 	winch := make(chan os.Signal, 1)
237: 	signal.Notify(winch, syscall.SIGWINCH)
238: 
239: 	go func() {
240: 		defer signal.Stop(winch)
241: 
242: 		for {
243: 			select {
244: 			case <-ctx.Done():
245: 				return
246: 			case <-winch:
247: 				send()
248: 			}
249: 		}
250: 	}()
251: 
252: 	return sizes
253: }

<content>
1: package cmd
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"context"
5: 	"os"
6: 	"os/signal"
7: 	"sync/atomic"
8: 	"syscall"
9: 	"time"
10: 
11: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
12: 	"github.com/spf13/cobra"
13: 	clablabruntime "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/labruntime"
14: )
15: 
16: const (
17: 	// maxCancelledDestroyTimeout bounds the rollback of a cancelled deployment on local
18: 	// runtimes, where removing what was created is a local and fast operation.
19: 	maxCancelledDestroyTimeout = 20 * time.Second
20: )
21: 
22: var (
23: 	// panics when closed twice, this way there can only be one signal handled context.
24: 	onlyOneSignalHandler = make(chan struct{}) //nolint: gochecknoglobals
25: 
26: 	// cancellationSignaled is closed once a SIGINT or SIGTERM has been received, letting
27: 	// commands tell a user-requested cancellation apart from an ordinary failure.
28: 	cancellationSignaled = make(chan struct{}) //nolint: gochecknoglobals
29: 
30: 	// restoreOnSignalExit restores a terminal a command set to raw mode before a signal ends
31: 	// the process.
32: 	restoreOnSignalExit atomic.Pointer[func()] //nolint: gochecknoglobals
33: )
34: 
35: // SignalHandledContext returns a context that will be canceled if a SIGINT or SIGTERM is
36: // received. Rolling back whatever the canceled operation had already created is the job of
37: // the command that owns it, so that its output stays ordered and the rollback cannot race
38: // with a deployment that is still unwinding. This handler only guarantees that the process
39: // still ends when there is nothing to roll back, or when the user signals a second time.
40: func SignalHandledContext() (context.Context, context.CancelFunc) {
41: 	// panics when called twice, this way there can only be one signal handled context
42: 	close(onlyOneSignalHandler)
43: 
44: 	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
45: 
46: 	sigs := make(chan os.Signal, 2) //nolint:mnd
47: 
48: 	signal.Notify(sigs, syscall.SIGINT, syscall.SIGTERM)
49: 
50: 	go func() {
51: 		sig := <-sigs
52: 
53: 		log.Errorf("received signal %q, canceling context", sig)
54: 
55: 		close(cancellationSignaled)
56: 
57: 		cancel()
58: 
59: 		options := GetOptions()
60: 
61: 		if options.Global.FinishOnCancel.Load() {
62: 			log.Info("Stopping, press Ctrl+C again to abort")
63: 
64: 			sig = <-sigs
65: 
66: 			log.Errorf("received signal %q while stopping, exiting", sig)
67: 
68: 			exitOnSignal()
69: 		}
70: 
71: 		if !options.Global.CleanOnCancel.Load() {
72: 			log.Debug("clean on cancel is not true, exiting")
73: 
74: 			exitOnSignal()
75: 		}
76: 
77: 		log.Info("Cleaning up the cancelled deployment, press Ctrl+C again to abort")
78: 
79: 		// The command performs the cleanup itself; exiting here would cut it short. A
80: 		// cleanup takes as long as the runtime needs to remove what was already created,
81: 		// so it is not put on a timer -- a second signal is the escape hatch instead.
82: 		sig = <-sigs
83: 
84: 		log.Errorf("received signal %q during cleanup, exiting", sig)
85: 
86: 		exitOnSignal()
87: 	}()
88: 
89: 	return ctx, cancel
90: }
91: 
92: // exitOnSignal ends the process after a signal, restoring the terminal first.
93: func exitOnSignal() {
94: 	if restore := restoreOnSignalExit.Load(); restore != nil {
95: 		(*restore)()
96: 	}
97: 
98: 	os.Exit(1)
99: }
100: 
101: // cancellationRequested reports whether a SIGINT or SIGTERM has been received.
102: func cancellationRequested() bool {
103: 	select {
104: 	case <-cancellationSignaled:
105: 		return true
106: 	default:
107: 		return false
108: 	}
109: }
110: 
111: // destroyCancelledDeploy removes the lab that a cancelled deployment was in the middle of
112: // creating. It runs on a fresh context because the command context is canceled by then.
113: func destroyCancelledDeploy(o *Options) {
114: 	destroyCtx, destroyCancel := context.WithTimeout(
115: 		context.Background(),
116: 		cancelledDestroyTimeout(o),
117: 	)
118: 	defer destroyCancel()
119: 
120: 	// destroyFn requires a cobra.Command but only needs the ctx from it
121: 	destroyCmd := &cobra.Command{}
122: 	destroyCmd.SetContext(destroyCtx)
123: 
124: 	err := destroyFn(destroyCmd, o)
125: 	if err != nil {
126: 		log.Errorf("failed destroying lab after cancellation signal: %v", err)
127: 	}
128: }
129: 
130: // cancelledDestroyTimeout bounds the rollback of a cancelled deployment. Controller-driven
131: // runtimes delete remote resources and wait for the cluster to converge on the deletion,
132: // which legitimately outlasts the bound that is generous for a local teardown.
133: func cancelledDestroyTimeout(o *Options) time.Duration {
134: 	if clablabruntime.IsLabRuntimeName(o.Global.Runtime) {
135: 		return o.Global.Timeout
136: 	}
137: 
138: 	return maxCancelledDestroyTimeout
139: }

(End of file - total 139 lines)

14: // Exec execute commands on running topology nodes.
15: func (c *CLab) Exec(
16: 	ctx context.Context,
17: 	cmds []string,
18: 	listOptions ...ListOption,
19: ) (*clabexec.ExecCollection, error) {
20: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
21: 		return c.execWithLabRuntime(ctx, cmds, listOptions...)
22: 	}
23: 
24: 	containers, err := c.ListContainers(ctx, listOptions...)
25: 	if err != nil {
26: 		return nil, err
27: 	}
28: 
29: 	// make sure filter returned containers
30: 	if len(containers) == 0 {
31: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("filter did not match any containers")
32: 	}
33: 
34: 	// prepare the exec collection and the exec command
35: 	resultCollection := clabexec.NewExecCollection()
36: 
37: 	// build execs from the string input
38: 	var execCmds []*clabexec.ExecCmd
39: 	for _, execCmdStr := range cmds {
40: 		execCmd, err := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromString(execCmdStr)
41: 		if err != nil {
42: 			return nil, err
43: 		}
44: 		execCmds = append(execCmds, execCmd)
45: 	}
46: 
47: 	// run the exec commands on all the containers matching the filter
48: 	for idx := range containers {
49: 		// iterate over the commands
50: 		for _, execCmd := range execCmds {
51: 			// execute the commands
52: 			execResult, err := containers[idx].RunExec(ctx, execCmd)
53: 			if err != nil {
54: 				// skip nodes that do not support exec
55: 				if errors.Is(err, clabexec.ErrRunExecNotSupported) {
56: 					continue
57: 				}
58: 				log.Warnf("exec on %s failed: %v", containers[idx].Names[0], err)
59: 				continue
60: 			}
61: 
62: 			resultCollection.Add(containers[idx].Names[0], execResult)
63: 		}
64: 	}
65: 
66: 	return resultCollection, nil
67: }
68: 
69: // ExecAttached runs a command in the container of the one node the list options select, with
70: // stdin, an optional TTY and terminal resizing, like docker exec -it. A command exiting nonzero
71: // returns a *labruntime.CommandExitError. With Docker and clabernetes, a command that can't
72: // start, such as a missing executable, returns an error without writing any output; Podman
73: // reports its exit code. Podman also sizes the terminal from containerlab's own terminal and
74: // ignores streams.Resize, and clabernetes applies the first size just after the command started.
75: //
76: // Stdin is read until it ends, which can outlast the call, so callers end it (for example by
77: // closing a pipe) once the session is over. With Docker, the end of stdin or the detach keys
78: // (ctrl-p, ctrl-q) end a terminal session without ending the command, which returns an error.
79: func (c *CLab) ExecAttached(
80: 	ctx context.Context,
81: 	cmd []string,
82: 	tty bool,
83: 	streams clablabruntime.AttachStreams,
84: 	listOptions ...ListOption,
85: ) error {
86: 	if len(cmd) == 0 {
87: 		return errors.New("an attached exec needs a command")
88: 	}
89: 
90: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
91: 		containers, err := c.ListLabRuntimeContainers(ctx, false, listOptions...)
92: 		if err != nil {
93: 			return err
94: 		}
95: 
96: 		target, err := attachedExecTarget(containers)
97: 		if err != nil {
98: 			return err
99: 		}
100: 
101: 		namespace, labName, nodeName, err := labRuntimeContainerParts(*target)
102: 		if err != nil {
103: 			return fmt.Errorf("exec target %s is invalid: %w", target.Names[0], err)
104: 		}
105: 
106: 		return c.AttachLabRuntimeNode(ctx, clablabruntime.AttachRequest{
107: 			Name:      labName,
108: 			Namespace: namespace,
109: 			NodeName:  nodeName,
110: 			Command:   cmd,
111: 			TTY:       tty,
112: 		}, streams)
113: 	}
114: 
115: 	containers, err := c.ListContainers(ctx, listOptions...)
116: 	if err != nil {
117: 		return err
118: 	}
119: 
120: 	target, err := attachedExecTarget(containers)
121: 	if err != nil {
122: 		return err
123: 	}
124: 
125: 	attacher, ok := target.Runtime.(clabruntime.ContainerAttacher)
126: 	if !ok {
127: 		return fmt.Errorf("container runtime %q does not support attached exec",
128: 			target.Runtime.GetName())
129: 	}
130: 
131: 	return attacher.AttachContainer(ctx, target.Names[0], cmd, tty, streams)
132: }
133: 
134: // attachedExecTarget returns the container of an attached exec, which needs the filter to match
135: // exactly one.
136: func attachedExecTarget(
137: 	containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer,
138: ) (*clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
139: 	switch len(containers) {
140: 	case 0:
141: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("filter did not match any containers")
142: 	case 1:
143: 		return &containers[0], nil
144: 	}
145: 
146: 	return nil, fmt.Errorf(
147: 		"an attached exec needs a filter matching exactly one container, the filter matched %d",
148: 		len(containers))
149: }

52: 	// UnPause / resume a container identified by its name

106: // ContainerLogOptions selects the part of a container log WriteContainerLogs writes.
107: type ContainerLogOptions struct {
108: 	// Tail limits the output to the last lines of the log; nil writes the whole log.
109: 	Tail *int64
110: 	// Follow keeps writing new log lines until the context ends.

112: 	// Timestamps prefixes every line with its timestamp.

141: }

143: // LogTail renders a log tail for container runtime APIs: "all" or the number of lines.
144: func LogTail(tail *int64) string {
145: 	if tail == nil || *tail < 0 {
146: 		return "all"
147: 	}
148: 
149: 	return strconv.FormatInt(*tail, 10)
150: }

166: // MgmtBridgeResolver is implemented by runtimes that can look up the bridge backing

168: type MgmtBridgeResolver interface {

170: 	// network exists and the bridge is not set yet. A missing network is not an error.
171: 	ResolveMgmtBridge(context.Context) error
172: }

Stdout then carries the combined output, and Resize applies.\n\tTTY      bool\n\tIdentity Identity\n}\n\n// AttachStreams connects an interactive session. Nil streams are not attached.\ntype AttachStreams struct {\n\tStdin  io.Reader\n\tStdout io.Writer\n\tStderr io.Writer\n\t// Resize delivers terminal size changes of a TTY session; closing it stops resizing.\n\tResize <-chan TerminalSize\n}\n\n// CommandExitError reports a command that ran and exited with a nonzero code.\ntype CommandExitError struct {\n\tCode int\n}\n\nfunc (e *CommandExitError) Error() string {\n\treturn fmt.Sprintf(\"command exited with code %d\", e.Code)\n}\n\n// LogRequest selects the node log StreamLogs writes.\ntype LogRequest struct {\n\t// Name is the lab name.\n\tName      string\n\tNamespace string\n\t// NodeName is the node whose log is streamed, as the topology or the runtime names it.\n\tNodeName string\n\t// Container overrides the node's primary device container.\n\tContainer string\n\t// Tail limits the output to the last lines of the log; nil writes the whole log.\n\tTail *int64\n\t// Follow keeps streaming new log lines until the context ends.\n\tFollow bool\n\t// Timestamps prefixes every line with its timestamp.\n\tTimestamps bool\n\tIdentity   Identity\n}\n\n// NodeStats is a resource usage sample of a node.\ntype NodeStats struct {\n\tNodeName  string\n\tNamespace string\n\tPod       string\n\t// CPUMilliCores is the CPU usage of the node's device containers, in thousandths of a core.\n\tCPUMilliCores int64\n\t// MemoryBytes is the memory working set of the node's device containers.\n\tMemoryBytes int64\n\t// Timestamp and Window describe the sampling interval the usage was measured over.\n\tTimestamp time.Time\n\tWindow    time.Duration\n}\n\n// ErrMetricsUnavailable reports a cluster without a resource metrics API (metrics.k8s.io), for\n// example one without metrics-server.\nvar ErrMetricsUnavailable = errors.New(\"metrics API (metrics.k8s.io) not available\")\n\n// ErrUnsupportedRuntimeVersion reports a cluster whose controller release lacks the contract a\n// lab runtime needs. The error that wraps it names the required release.\nvar ErrUnsupportedRuntimeVersion = errors.New(\"unsupported lab runtime controller version\")\n\n// SupportChecker checks that the cluster behind a lab runtime runs a controller release with\n// the contract the runtime needs. Lab runtimes run the check on their own before the first\n// cluster call of an operation; servers call it to fail early, for example at startup. It returns\n// an error wrapping ErrUnsupportedRuntimeVersion when the release is too old.\ntype SupportChecker interface {\n\tCheckSupport(ctx context.Context, identity Identity) error\n}\n\n// NodeLocator resolves lab nodes to the workloads that run them. Empty NodeRequest.Nodes\n// locates every node of the lab.\ntype NodeLocator interface {\n\tLocateNode(ctx context.Context, req NodeRequest) ([]NodeLocation, error)\n}\n\n// NodeAttacher runs an interactive command (stdin, TTY and resize) in a node's container. It\n// returns a *CommandExitError when the command exits with a nonzero code.\ntype NodeAttacher interface {\n\tAttach(ctx context.Context, req AttachRequest, streams AttachStreams) error\n}\n\n// NodeLogStreamer writes a node container's log.\ntype NodeLogStreamer interface {\n\tStreamLogs(ctx context.Context, req LogRequest, w io.Writer) error\n}\n\n// NodePortForwarder opens a stream to a TCP port of a node (SSH, web UIs). 18: // them or as a lab runtime sanitized them. Lab runtime calls act as the identity set with

21: // CheckLabRuntimeSupport checks that the cluster behind the lab runtime runs a controller release
22: // with the contract the runtime needs (for c9s: clabernetes with Node run state and the clabwire
23: // capture contract). Lab runtime operations run the same check before their first cluster call;
24: // servers call it to fail early. The error wraps labruntime.ErrUnsupportedRuntimeVersion when the
25: // release is too old.
26: func (c *CLab) CheckLabRuntimeSupport(ctx context.Context) error {
27: 	checker, err := labRuntimeCapability[clablabruntime.SupportChecker](
28: 		c, "checking the lab runtime version")
29: 	if err != nil {
30: 		return err
31: 	}
32:

39: 	ctx context.Context,

125: // NodeLogOptions selects the node log NodeLogs writes.
126: type NodeLogOptions struct {
127: 	// NodeName is the node whose log is written.
128: 	NodeName string
129: 	// Container overrides a lab runtime node's primary device container. Local runtimes do
130: 	// not support it.
131: 	Container string
132: 	// Tail limits the output to the last lines of the log; nil writes the whole log.
133: 	Tail *int64
134: 	// Follow keeps writing new log lines until the context ends.
135: 	Follow bool
136: 	// Timestamps prefixes every line with its timestamp.
137: 	Timestamps bool
138: }
139: 
140: // NodeLogs writes the log of a lab node with every runtime: the container log with Docker and
141: // Podman, the device container log with a lab runtime. Lab runtimes write one combined stream to
142: // stdout.
143: func (c *CLab) NodeLogs(
144: 	ctx context.Context,
145: 	opts NodeLogOptions,
146: 	stdout, stderr io.Writer,
147: ) error {
148: 	if opts.NodeName == "" {
149: 		return fmt.Errorf("node name is required")
150: 	}
151: 	if err := c.requireLabName(); err != nil {
152: 		return err
153: 	}
154: 
155: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
156: 		streamer, err := labRuntimeCapability[clablabruntime.NodeLogStreamer](
157: 			c, "streaming node logs")
158: 		if err != nil {
159: 			return err
160: 		}
161: 
162: 		return streamer.StreamLogs(ctx, clablabruntime.LogRequest{
163: 			Name:       c.Config.Name,
164: 			NodeName:   opts.NodeName,
165: 			Container:  opts.Container,
166: 			Tail:       opts.Tail,
167: 			Follow:     opts.Follow,
168: 			Timestamps: opts.Timestamps,
169: 			Identity:   c.labRuntimeIdentity,
170: 		}, stdout)
171: 	}
172: 
173: 	if opts.Container != "" {
174: 		return fmt.Errorf("selecting a container is only supported with lab runtimes")
175: 	}
176: 
177: 	runtime, containerName, err := c.nodeContainer(ctx, opts.NodeName)
178: 	if err != nil {
179: 		return err
180: 	}
181: 
182: 	writer, ok := runtime.(clabruntime.ContainerLogWriter)
183: 	if !ok {
184: 		return fmt.Errorf("container runtime %q does not support node logs", runtime.GetName())
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	return writer.WriteContainerLogs(ctx, containerName, clabruntime.ContainerLogOptions{
188: 		Tail:       opts.Tail,
189: 		Follow:     opts.Follow,
190: 		Timestamps: opts.Timestamps,
191: 	}, stdout, stderr)
192: }
193: 
194: // nodeContainer finds the container of a lab node in the local container runtimes by the lab
195: // and node name labels.
196: func (c *CLab) nodeContainer(
197: 	ctx context.Context,
198: 	nodeName string,
199: ) (clabruntime.ContainerRuntime, string, error) {
200: 	filters := []*clabtypes.GenericFilter{
201: 		{
202: 			FilterType: "label",
203: 			Field:      clabconstants.Containerlab,
204: 			Operator:   "=",
205: 			Match:      c.Config.Name,
206: 		},
207: 		{
208: 			FilterType: "label",
209: 			Field:      clabconstants.NodeName,
210: 			Operator:   "=",
211: 			Match:      nodeName,
212: 		},
213: 	}
214: 
215: 	runtimeNames := make([]string, 0, len(c.Runtimes))
216: 	for name := range c.Runtimes {
217: 		runtimeNames = append(runtimeNames, name)
218: 	}
219: 	sort.Strings(runtimeNames)
220: 
221: 	for _, name := range runtimeNames {
222: 		runtime := c.Runtimes[name]
223: 
224: 		containers, err := runtime.ListContainers(ctx, filters)
225: 		if err != nil {
226: 			return nil, "", fmt.Errorf("could not list containers: %w", err)
227: 		}
228: 		if len(containers) != 0 && len(containers[0].Names) != 0 {
229: 			return runtime, containers[0].Names[0], nil
230: 		}
231: 	}
232: 
233: 	return nil, "", fmt.Errorf("%w: node %q of lab %q has no container",
234: 		ErrNodeNotFound, nodeName, c.Config.Name)
235: }
236: 
237: func (c *CLab) requireLabName() error {
238: 	if c.Config == nil || c.Config.Name == "" {
239: 		return fmt.Errorf("provide a lab name (--name) or a topology file (--topo)")
240: 	}
241: 
242: 	return nil
243: }
244: 
245: // labRuntimeCapability returns the CLab's lab runtime as an optional lab runtime interface.
246: func labRuntimeCapability[T any](c *CLab, operation string) (T, error) {
247: 	var zero T
248: 
249: 	if c.LabRuntime == nil {
250: 		return zero, fmt.Errorf("%s needs a lab runtime such as %q",
251: 			operation, clablabruntime.ClabernetesRuntimeName)
252: 	}
253: 
254: 	capability, ok := c.LabRuntime.(T)
255: 	if !ok {
256: 		return zero, fmt.Errorf("lab runtime %q does not support %s",
257: 			c.globalRuntimeName, operation)
258: 	}
259: 
260: 	return capability, nil
261: }

2233: // execStartCheck holds back the first output of an exec until Docker reports whether the command
2234: // started. Docker writes why a command didn't start into the output, which becomes the error
2235: // instead. Output after the session ended is dropped.
2236: type execStartCheck struct {

2238: 
2239: 	mu      sync.Mutex
2240: 	checked bool
2241: 	failed  bool

2251: }

2254: 	return execStartWriter{check: c, w: w}

2259: 	defer c.mu.Unlock()

2261: 	return c.failed, string(c.reason)

2264: // hold tells whether output must be held back or dropped, checking the exec on the first output.
2265: func (c *execStartCheck) hold(p []byte) bool {
2266: 	c.mu.Lock()
2267: 	defer c.mu.Unlock()
2268: 
2269: 	if c.ended {
2270: 		return true
2271: 	}
2272: 	if !c.checked {
2273: 		c.checked = true
2274: 		if inspect, err := c.inspect(); err == nil && !inspect.Running && inspect.Pid == 0 {
2275: 			c.failed = true
2276: 		}
2277: 	}
2278: 	if c.failed {
2279: 		c.reason = append(c.reason, p...)
2280: 	}
2281: 
2282: 	return c.failed
2283: }

795: // AttachContainer runs an interactive command in a container through a Podman exec session.
796: // The Podman bindings run a terminal session from the process's own terminal, which they set
797: // to raw mode and take the size from, so streams.Resize is not used.
798: func (r *PodmanRuntime) AttachContainer(
799: 	ctx context.Context,
800: 	containerName string,
801: 	cmd []string,
802: 	tty bool,
803: 	streams labruntime.AttachStreams,
804: ) error {
805: 	if tty && term.IsTerminal(int(os.Stdin.Fd())) && streams.Stdin != os.Stdin {
806: 		return errors.New("podman connects a terminal session to containerlab's terminal, " +
807: 			"so the session needs containerlab's stdin")
808: 	}
809: 
810: 	ctx, err := r.connect(ctx)
811: 	if err != nil {
812: 		return err
813: 	}
814: 
815: 	execCreateConf := handlers.ExecCreateConfig{}
816: 	execCreateConf.User = "root"
817: 	execCreateConf.Tty = tty
818: 	execCreateConf.AttachStdin = streams.Stdin != nil
819: 	execCreateConf.AttachStdout = streams.Stdout != nil
820: 	execCreateConf.AttachStderr = streams.Stderr != nil
821: 	execCreateConf.Cmd = cmd
822: 	execID, err := containers.ExecCreate(ctx, containerName, &execCreateConf)
823: 	if err != nil {
824: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create exec in container %q: %w", containerName, err)
825: 	}
826: 
827: 	execSAAOpts := new(containers.ExecStartAndAttachOptions)
828: 	if streams.Stdin != nil {
829: 		execSAAOpts.WithInputStream(*bufio.NewReader(streams.Stdin)).WithAttachInput(true)
830: 	}
831: 	if streams.Stdout != nil {
832: 		execSAAOpts.WithOutputStream(streams.Stdout).WithAttachOutput(true)
833: 	}
834: 	if streams.Stderr != nil {
835: 		execSAAOpts.WithErrorStream(streams.Stderr).WithAttachError(true)
836: 	}
837: 
838: 	if err := containers.ExecStartAndAttach(ctx, execID, execSAAOpts); err != nil {
839: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to start/attach exec in container %q: %w", containerName, err)
840: 	}
841: 
842: 	inspectOut, err := containers.ExecInspect(ctx, execID, nil)
843: 	if err != nil {
844: 		return err
845: 	}
846: 	if inspectOut.ExitCode != 0 {
847: 		return &labruntime.CommandExitError{Code: inspectOut.ExitCode}
848: 	}
849: 
850: 	return nil
851: }

853: func (*PodmanRuntime) StreamEvents(
854: 	context.Context,

220: // newExecutor streams a pod exec over websockets, falling back to SPDY when the API server
221: // does not upgrade the websocket, like kubectl. The websocket executor reports upgrade
222: // failures with the k8s.io/streaming error types. Both transports are built from the runtime's
223: // rest config, so they carry its impersonation headers.
224: func (r *Runtime) newExecutor(execURL *url.URL) (remotecommand.Executor, error) {
225: 	websocketExecutor, err := remotecommand.NewWebSocketExecutor(
226: 		r.restConfig, http.MethodGet, execURL.String())
227: 	if err != nil {
228: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create Kubernetes websocket exec executor: %w", err)
229: 	}
230: 
231: 	spdyExecutor, err := remotecommand.NewSPDYExecutor(r.restConfig, http.MethodPost, execURL)
232: 	if err != nil {
233: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create Kubernetes exec executor: %w", err)
234: 	}
235: 
236: 	return remotecommand.NewFallbackExecutor(websocketExecutor, spdyExecutor,
237: 		func(err error) bool {
238: 			return streamhttp.IsUpgradeFailure(err) || streamhttp.IsHTTPSProxyError(err)
239: 		})
240: }

242: // terminalSizeQueue adapts a resize channel to the remotecommand size queue.
243: type terminalSizeQueue struct {
244: 	ctx    context.Context
245: 	resize <-chan clablabruntime.TerminalSize
246: }

248: func newTerminalSizeQueue(
249: 	ctx context.Context,
250: 	resize <-chan clablabruntime.TerminalSize,
251: ) *terminalSizeQueue {
252: 	return &terminalSizeQueue{ctx: ctx, resize: resize}
253: }
